It’s gotten bad, and I’m scared I can’t stop doing it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: For at least the last three months, I don’t think there’s been a week where it didn’t happen. Usually it’s after a late-night eating episode, and then I go make myself throw up. Some weeks it’s several times, especially if I’m home more because of my back.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel like you've eaten an excessive amount of food before making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s usually at night, after everyone’s gone quiet. I’ll end up in the kitchen eating leftovers, cereal, chips, ice cream—whatever’s there—and I keep going way past a normal meal. By the time I stop, it feels like half the pantry’s been picked over.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating those large amounts of food at night, do you feel like you can't stop yourself or control how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Once I start, it feels like I’m not really in control anymore. I’ll even be thinking, “Lucy, stop, knock it off,” but my hands just keep grabbing more food. I don’t feel back in charge until after it’s already over.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight when you're not making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Yes. If the scale is even up a pound, I panic. I start thinking my body’s getting soft from being laid up with my back, and I’m already trying to figure out how to undo it before I can even calm down.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up only after these episodes of eating a lot at night?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yeah. It happens after I’ve eaten too much and I get scared I’m going to gain weight. I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. It’s gotten to where I’m scared I can’t stop doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without making yourself throw up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. At night, after everybody’s gone quiet, I’ll be in the kitchen and just keep eating—leftovers, cereal, chips, ice cream, whatever’s there. It’s way more than a normal meal, and by the time I stop it feels like half the pantry’s been picked through.\n\nDoctor: Do you also use other methods to try to prevent weight gain, such as excessive exercise or strict dieting?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. Usually it’s after a late-night eating episode, and then I go make myself throw up. If the scale is even up a pound, I panic. I start thinking my body’s getting soft from being laid up with my back, and I’m already trying to figure out how to undo it before I can even calm down.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up only after these episodes of eating a lot at night?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yeah. It happens after I’ve eaten too much and I get scared I’m going to gain weight. I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. It’s way more than a normal meal, and by the time I stop it feels like half the pantry’s been picked through.\n\nDoctor: Do you also use other methods to try to prevent weight gain, such as excessive exercise or strict dieting?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. It’s more that after I eat too much, I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up so I don’t gain weight. There might be some nights I don’t, but I haven’t really kept track.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: For at least the last three months, it’s been almost every week. Honestly, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t do it after eating like that at night. Some weeks it happens several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up after eating a normal meal, not just after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes I do. It’s usually worse after I’ve eaten too much, but even after a regular meal, if I start worrying I’m going to gain weight, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. I don’t like saying it, but it’s gotten hard to stop.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating a normal meal?\n\nPatient: It happens a lot, honestly. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… after I eat too much, I’ve been making myself throw up in the bathroom so I don’t gain weight. It’s gotten bad, and I’m scared I can’t stop doing it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: For at least the last three months, I don’t think there’s been a week where it didn’t happen. Usually it’s after a late-night eating episode, and then I go make myself throw up. Some weeks it’s several times, especially if I’m home more because of my back.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel like you've eaten an excessive amount of food before making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s usually at night, after everyone’s gone quiet. I’ll end up in the kitchen eating leftovers, cereal, chips, ice cream—whatever’s there—and I keep going way past a normal meal. By the time I stop, it feels like half the pantry’s been picked over.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating those large amounts of food at night, do you feel like you can't stop yourself or control how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Once I start, it feels like I’m not really in control anymore. I’ll even be thinking, “Lucy, stop, knock it off,” but my hands just keep grabbing more food. I don’t feel back in charge until after it’s already over.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight when you're not making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Yes. If the scale is even up a pound, I panic. I start thinking my body’s getting soft from being laid up with my back, and I’m already trying to figure out how to undo it before I can even calm down.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up only after these episodes of eating a lot at night?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yeah. It happens after I’ve eaten too much and I get scared I’m going to gain weight. I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. It’s gotten to where I’m scared I can’t stop doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without making yourself throw up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. At night, after everybody’s gone quiet, I’ll be in the kitchen and just keep eating—leftovers, cereal, chips, ice cream, whatever’s there. It’s way more than a normal meal, and by the time I stop it feels like half the pantry’s been picked through.\n\nDoctor: Do you also use other methods to try to prevent weight gain, such as excessive exercise or strict dieting?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I think about cutting back on food, but I’m not sure I’d call it strict dieting. And with my back, I’m not doing a bunch of exercise or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever go on strict diets or fast for periods of time to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I go days without eating or anything like that. I’ll tell myself I need to cut back, but I don’t know if I’d call it a strict diet.\n\nDoctor: Do you use laxatives or diuretics to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: No, not laxatives or water pills. It’s more that after I eat too much, I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up so I don’t gain weight. It’s gotten bad, and I’m scared I can’t stop doing it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have these episodes of eating a lot at night without making yourself throw up afterwards?\n\nPatient: That happens at night, after everyone’s gone quiet. I’ll be in the kitchen and just keep eating—leftovers, cereal, chips, ice cream, whatever’s there. It goes way past a normal meal, like I’ll look around and half the pantry’s been picked over.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have these episodes of eating a lot at night without making yourself throw up afterwards?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how often it happens without me throwing up after. Most of the time if I eat like that, I end up trying to get rid of it. There might be some nights I don’t, but I haven’t really kept track.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: For at least the last three months, it’s been almost every week. Honestly, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t do it after eating like that at night. Some weeks it happens several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up after eating a normal meal, not just after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes I do. It’s usually worse after I’ve eaten too much, but even after a regular meal, if I start worrying I’m going to gain weight, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. I don’t like saying it, but it’s gotten hard to stop.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating a normal meal?\n\nPatient: It happens a lot, honestly. If I eat a normal meal and then start feeling scared I’m going to gain weight, I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. I don’t know the exact number, but it’s gotten to where I’m scared I can’t stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up after eating, on average, at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes. For the past few months, it’s been at least once a week. Honestly, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t have one of those late-night eating episodes and then make myself throw up afterward. Some weeks it’s more than once, especially when I’m stuck at home more because of my back.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say you make yourself throw up after eating a normal meal, not just after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’d say… probably several times a month, at least. It usually happens after I feel like I ate too much, and I go make myself throw up because I’m scared of gaining weight. It’s gotten to where I’m honestly scared I can’t stop.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many times a month do you make yourself throw up after eating a normal meal?\n\nPatient: On average… I’d say maybe around six or eight times a month after a normal meal. I’m honestly scared I can’t stop doing it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I don’t really fast, no. I might skip a meal here and there if I’m upset or busy, but I don’t set out to go long periods without eating to control my weight.

Some weeks it’s several times, especially if I’m home more because of my back.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel like you've eaten an excessive amount of food before making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s usually at night, after everyone’s gone quiet. I’ll end up in the kitchen eating leftovers, cereal, chips, ice cream—whatever’s there—and I keep going way past a normal meal. By the time I stop, it feels like half the pantry’s been picked over.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating those large amounts of food at night, do you feel like you can't stop yourself or control how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Once I start, it feels like I’m not really in control anymore. I’ll even be thinking, “Lucy, stop, knock it off,” but my hands just keep grabbing more food. I don’t feel back in charge until after it’s already over.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight when you're not making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Yes. If the scale is even up a pound, I panic. I start thinking my body’s getting soft from being laid up with my back, and I’m already trying to figure out how to undo it before I can even calm down.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up only after these episodes of eating a lot at night?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yeah. It happens after I’ve eaten too much and I get scared I’m going to gain weight. I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. It’s gotten to where I’m scared I can’t stop doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without making yourself throw up afterwards?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. At night, after everybody’s gone quiet, I’ll be in the kitchen and just keep eating—leftovers, cereal, chips, ice cream, whatever’s there. It’s way more than a normal meal, and by the time I stop it feels like half the pantry’s been picked through.\n\nDoctor: Do you also use other methods to try to prevent weight gain, such as excessive exercise or strict dieting?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I think about cutting back on food, but I’m not sure I’d call it strict dieting. I don’t think I go days without eating or anything like that. I’ll be in the kitchen and just keep eating—leftovers, cereal, chips, ice cream, whatever’s there. It goes way past a normal meal, like I’ll look around and half the pantry’s been picked over.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have these episodes of eating a lot at night without making yourself throw up afterwards?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how often it happens without me throwing up after. Most of the time if I eat like that, I end up trying to get rid of it. It usually happens after I feel like I ate too much, and I go make myself throw up because I’m scared of gaining weight.
